Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n    12→\n    13→3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n    30→- **Documentation** on nwb file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io/en/latest/\n    31→\n    32→## Decoder Task\n    33→\n    34→Temporally align based on start of the trial. \n    35→\n    36→### Decoder Inputs:\n    37→- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    38→- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n    39→- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n    40→- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n    41→\n    42→### Decoder Outputs\n    43→- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n    44→  - 0:  < -50 cm\n    45→  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n    46→  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    47→  - 3: 0 cm\n    48→  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n    49→  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n    50→  - 6: > +50 cm\n    51→- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n    52→- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n    53→  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n    54→  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n    55→  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n    56→  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n    57→  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n    58→- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    59→- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n    60→- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    61→\n    62→## Target Data Format\n    63→\n    64→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    65→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    66→\n    67→```python\n    68→data = {\n    69→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    70→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    71→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    72→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    73→            ...\n    74→        ],\n    75→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    76→            ...\n    77→        ],\n    78→        ...\n    79→    ],\n    80→\n    81→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    82→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    83→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    84→            ...\n    85→        ],\n    86→        ...\n    87→    ],\n    88→\n    89→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    90→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    91→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    92→            ...\n    93→        ],\n    94→        ...\n    95→    ],\n    96→\n    97→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    98→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    99→\n   100→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n   101→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n   102→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n   103→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n   104→\n   105→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n   106→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n   107→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n   108→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n   109→        ...\n   110→    ],\n   111→\n   112→    'metadata': {\n   113→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   114→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   115→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   116→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   117→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   118→        ...\n   119→    }\n   120→}\n   121→```\n   122→\n   123→### Key Format Requirements\n   124→\n   125→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   126→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   127→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   128→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   129→\n   130→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   131→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   132→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   133→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   134→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   135→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   136→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   137→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   138→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   139→\n   140→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   141→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   142→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   143→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   144→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   145→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   146→\n   147→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   148→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   149→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   150→\n   151→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   152→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   153→\n   154→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   155→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   156→\n   157→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   158→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   159→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   160→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   161→\n   162→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   163→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   164→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   165→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   166→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   167→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   168→\n   169→## Python environment\n   170→\n   171→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   172→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   173→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   174→\n   175→## Decoder Reference\n   176→\n   177→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   178→- Optional arguments:\n   179→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   180→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   181→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   182→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   183→\n   184→### Purpose\n   185→\n   186→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   187→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   188→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   189→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   190→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   191→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   192→\n   193→### Consistency requirements\n   194→\n   195→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   196→- Loading of data\n   197→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   198→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   199→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   200→\n   201→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   202→\n   203→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   204→\n   205→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   206→\n   207→Carefully verify your work after every step. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n- **Documentation** on nwb file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io/en/latest/\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference paper and code and these instructions. During testing, the reference version of `train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. n_full = x.shape[0] // factor\n    out = []\n    if n_full:\n        out.append(np.any(x[: n_full * factor].reshape(n_full, factor) > 0, axis=1))\n    if x.shape[0] % factor:\n out[distance_cm < -50.0] = 0\n    out[(distance_cm >= -50.0) & (distance_cm < -10.0)] = 1\n    out[(distance_cm >= -10.0) & (distance_cm < 0.0)] = 2\n    out[distance_cm == 0.0] = 3\n    out[(distance_cm > 0.0) & (distance_cm <= 10.0)] = 4\n    out[(distance_cm > 10.0) & (distance_cm <= 50.0)] = 5\n    out[distance_cm > 50.0] = 6\n    return out\n\n\ndef discretize_position(position_cm: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    clipped = np.clip(position_cm, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM - 1e-6)\n    return np.minimum((clipped / (TRACK_LENGTH_CM / 5.0)).astype(np.int64), 4)\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed_cm_s: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.zeros(speed_cm_s.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n    out[(speed_cm_s >= 2.0) & (speed_cm_s < 10.0)] = 1\n    out[(speed_cm_s >= 10.0) & (speed_cm_s < 20.0)] = 2\n    out[(speed_cm_s >= 20.0) & (speed_cm_s < 40.0)] = 3\n    out[speed_cm_s >= 40.0] = 4\n    return out\n\n\ndef compute_distance_to_zone(position_cm: np.ndarray, zone_idx: int) -> np.ndarray:\n    zone_start, zone_end = ZONE_BOUNDS[zone_idx]\n    distance = np.zeros(position_cm.shape, dtype=np.float32)\n    before = position_cm < zone_start\n    after = position_cm > zone_end\n    distance[before] = position_cm[before] - zone_start\n    distance[after] = position_cm[after] - zone_end\n    return    16→- `data/`\n    17→- `decoder.py`\n    18→- `docker-compose.yaml`\n    19→- `methods.txt`\n    20→- `paper.pdf`\n    21→- `train_decoder.py`\n    22→\n    23→Environment checks:\n    24→- `python3` runs successfully\n    25→- `numpy` import verified: `2.3.5`\n    26→- `torch` import verified: `2.6.0+cu124`\n    27→- `ls -la CONVERSION_NOTES.md` confirmed the file exists before proceeding\n    28→\n    29→---\n    30→\n    31→## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n    32→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    33→\n    34→### Key Functions Identified\n    35→| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n    36→|----------|------|-------|---------|\n    37→| `create_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | LOADING | Creates a `TwoPUtils.sess.Session` object from imaging + VR files, then appends aligned session data. |\n    38→| `append_session_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns VR to imaging, loads suite2p outputs, adds timeseries (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`) and position-binned trial matrices. |\n    39→| `dff` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | Computes trial-restricted dF/F with neuropil subtraction, `maximin` baseline, smoothing, and optional OASIS deconvolution to `events`. |\n    40→| `multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | LOADING | Loads saved `sess` pickles across animals for an experiment day and attaches dF/F, events, place-cell outputs, reward labels, and trial subsets. |\n    41→| `quick_load_multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | LOADING | Loads already processed multi-animal pickle files from the shared preprocessed data location. |\n    42→| `get_trial_types` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Computes per-trial reward outcome and environment morph from aligned VR data between `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. |\n    43→| `get_reward_zones` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Maps scene names to reward-zone coordinates and labels (`A`, `B`, `C`) for each trial, including switch sessions. |\n    44→| `define_trial_subsets` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Splits trials into trial sets based on reward-zone switches or halves for single-zone sessions. |\n    45→| `get_timeseries_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py` | PROCESSING | Builds continuously sampled decoder-ready behavior and neural arrays from `sess.timeseries['events']`, masking low-speed and invalid samples. |\n    46→| `CircularRegression` | `code/src/reward_relative/decode.py` | PROCESSING | Paper decoder model for circular variables; confirms the reference decoding was done on neural activity against position-like targets. |\n    47→\n    48→### Notes\n    49→- The reference repo is for **2-photon calcium imaging**, not electrophysiology. There is no spike sorting or ephys quality filtering in this code path.\n    50→- `sess` is the base aligned session object. The README explicitly states raw `sess` objects **do not contain dF/F**; dF/F is computed later.\n    51→- `make_session_pkl.md` shows the first saved object is `sess`, created from raw suite2p + VR files after manual suite2p curation.\n    52→- Cell curation is manual in the suite2p GUI; `iscell.npy` is the curated cell mask used when loading suite2p outputs.\n    53→- `multi_anim_sess` is the main post-processing stage used by the paper:\n    54→  - loads saved `sess` pickle for each animal/day,\n    55→  - computes dF/F,\n    56→  - optionally deconvolves dF/F into `events`,\n    57→  - adds position-binned trial matrices,\n    58→  - computes place-cell statistics and trial subset metadata.\n    59→- For single-channel data, `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)` calls `preprocessing.dff(...)` with:\n    60→  - `neuropil_method='subtract'`\n    61→  - `baseline_method='maximin'`\n    62→  - `subtract_baseline=True`\n    63→  - `neu_coef=0.7`\n    64→  - `deconvolve=True`\n    65→  - optional `keep_teleports` per animal\n    66→- `dff` restricts valid fluorescence to trial spans using `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. Baseline is computed **within each trial** after smoothing, min-filtering, and max-filtering. The resulting dF/F is smoothed again, then OASIS is applied to get `events`.\n    67→- `append_session_data` adds behavioral timeseries aligned to imaging frames. The pipeline is therefore based on **frame-aligned** neural and behavior streams.\n    68→- `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` is the clearest reference for paper-style decoder inputs:\n    69→  - neural data are `sess.timeseries['events']`\n    70→  - trial boundaries use `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`\n    71→  - reward-relative position is computed per sample from the reward-zone start for that trial\n    72→  - low-speed samples are masked out with `use_speed_thr=2`\n    73→  - lick sensor artifacts are suppressed when cumulative licks are implausibly high\n    74→  - samples with NaNs in neural data or masked speed/lick channels are dropped\n    75→- Reward-zone labels come from `behavior.get_reward_zones`:\n    76→  - `A` corresponds to zone `X` = `[80, 130]`\n    77→  - `B` corresponds to zone `Y` = `[200, 250]`\n    78→  - `C` corresponds to zone `Z` = `[320, 370]`\n    79→  - environment names remain `Env1`, `Env2`, `Env3`\n    80→- The Fig. 3 decoder notebook uses:\n    81→  - experiment `MetaLearn`\n    82→  - experiment days `[3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14]`\n    83→  - `ts_key='dff'` when loading saved `multiDayData`, but continuous neural decoder inputs are assembled from `sess.timeseries['events']` via `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n    84→  - occupancy matching by reward-relative position bin for some paper analyses\n    85→- Conclusion for conversion:\n    86→  - **Yes, dF/F must be computed** to match the reference processing.\n    87→  - The decoder-relevant neural stream is most likely the **deconvolved `events`**, not raw fluorescence.\n    88→  - Trial alignment should use `trial_start_inds` as the start and `teleport_inds` as the end of each trial.\n    89→\n    90→---\n    91→\n    92→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    93→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    94→\n    95→### Data Structure\n    96→- `data/` contains one NWB file per subject-session plus `dandiset.yaml`.\n    97→- Subject directories present:\n    98→  - `sub-m3`, `sub-m4`, `sub-m7`, `sub-m11`, `sub-m12`, `sub-m13`, `sub-m14`, `sub-m15`, `sub-m17`, `sub-m18`, `sub-m19`\n    99→- File pattern:\n   100→  - `data/sub-<mouse>/sub-<mouse>_ses-<NN>_behavior+ophys.nwb`\n   101→- Total NWB files: `152`\n   102→- Sessions per subject from file inventory:\n   103→  - `sub-m11`: 12 sessions (`ses-03` to `ses-14`)\n   104→  - all other listed subjects: 14 sessions each\n   105→- Each NWB contains:\n   106→  - `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries`\n   107→  - `processing/ophys/Fluorescence`\n   108→  - `processing/ophys/Neuropil`\n   109→  - `processing/ophys/Deconvolved`\n   110→  - `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation`\n   111→- Behavioral time series present in every sampled file:\n   112→  - `Reward`\n   113→  - `autoreward`\n   114→  - `environment`\n   115→  - `lick`\n   116→  - `position`\n   117→  - `reward_zone`\n   118→  - `scanning`\n   119→  - `speed`\n   120→  - `teleport`\n   121→  - `trial number`\n   122→  - `trial_start`\n   123→- Ophys organization in sample NWB:\n   124→  - `Deconvolved/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n   125→  - `Fluorescence/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n   126→  - `Neuropil/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n   127→  - `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation` columns:\n   128→    - `pixel_mask`\n   129→    - `iscell`\n   130→    - `planeIdx`\n   131→- `iscell` has shape `(n_rois, 2)` and matches suite2p format:\n   132→  - column 0: binary cell flag (`0` or `1`)\n   133→  - column 1: suite2p confidence/probability\n   134→- Imaging metadata from sample file:\n   135→  - indicator: `GCaMP7f`\n   136→  - location: `hippocampus, CA1`\n   137→- Neural sampling rates present across the dataset:\n   138→  - `15.5078125 Hz` in 124 sessions\n   139→  - `31.015625 Hz` in 28 sessions\n   140→- Frame-count alignment:\n   141→  - most sessions have equal behavior/neural frame counts\n   142→  - 10 sessions are off by exactly 1 sample (`behavior = neural - 1`), so edge handling will be required\n   143→- Behavioral coding observed directly from NWB values:\n   144→  - `environment`: values `-1, 0, 1` (`-1` appears outside valid trial/imaging periods)\n   145→  - `trial number`: values `-1` plus contiguous nonnegative trial ids\n   146→  - `trial_start`, `teleport`, `lick`, `scanning`: binary-like 0/1 signals with some `-1` outside valid periods\n   147→  - `Reward`: sparse event series with separate timestamps (not frame-length)\n   148→\n   149→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n   150→| Statistic | Value |\n   151→|-----------|-------|\n   152→| Reference Text Reading\n   162→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   163→\n   164→### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   165→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   166→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   167→| Neurons (total) | Not directly reported | Methods report per-session counts rather than a total. |\n   168→| Neurons / session | `155–2172` putative pyramidal neurons/session | “155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session” |\n   169→| Subjects | `11` switch-task mice in main dataset; `3` fixed-condition mice in separate cohort | “switch task (n = 11 mice)” and “fixed-condition cohort (n = 3 mice)” |\n   170→| Sessions / subject | `14` imaging days for task; `m11` starts imaging on day 3 | “total of 14 days” and “for m11, imaging started on day 3” |\n   171→| Trials (total) | `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (for licking QC reference) | “81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice” |\n   172→| Trials / session | target `80–100`; mean `80.5 ± 7.4` across all imaging days | “We targeted 80–100 trials per session… mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials” |\n   173→| Neural data time bin | imaging sampled at `~15.5 Hz` per plane | “sampled at ~15.5 Hz” / “~15.5 Hz per plane” |\n   174→| Behavior data time bin | behavior aligned to imaging frame rate `~15.5 Hz` | “All behavioral and neural time series were sampled at ~15.5 Hz” |\n   175→| Reward rate | `~85% rewarded`, `~15% omitted` | “Reward was randomly omitted on approximately 15% of trials” |\n   176→| Reward switch timing | switch after `30` trials | “Each switch occurred after 30 trials” |\n   177→| Reward zones | A=`80–130 cm`, B=`200–250 cm`, C=`320–370 cm` | “zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm” |\n   178→| Track length | `450 cm` | “450 cm linear track” |\n   179→| Decoder analysis set | `77 sessions`, `11 mice`, `7` switch days | “n = 77 sessions, 11 mice, seven switch days” |\n   180→| Decoder general result | before-switch: RR/TR/non-RR above shuffle; after-switch: only RR above shuffle | “when we tested on trials after the reward switch, only the RR population decoded… better than shuffle” |\n   181→| Decoder spatial extent | z-scored decode `>2` from `−104.5 ± 20.1 cm` to `+152.7 ± 22.9 cm` relative to reward | “range of z-scored decode >2: −104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm” |\n   182→| GLM performance reference | FDE all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` | “On average, the model’s FDE…” |\n   183→\n   184→\n   185→### Processing Details\n   186→- Task structure:\n   187→  - hidden 50 cm reward zone on a 450 cm linear VR track\n   188→  - two main environments (`ENV 1`, `ENV 2`) in the switch task\n   189→  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n   190→  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n   191→  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n   192→  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n   193→- Imaging / alignment:\n   194→  - 2-photon CA1 calcium imaging with GCaMP7f\n   195→  - most sessions sampled at ~15.5 Hz\n   196→  - m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes interleaved at ~31 Hz, yielding ~15.5 Hz per plane\n   197→  - imaging sometimes stopped during teleport periods to minimize photodamage; some animals/days were imaged through teleports\n   198→- Neural preprocessing:\n   199→  - Suite2P used for motion correction and ROI detection\n   200→  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n   201→  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n   202→  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n   203→  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n   204→- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n   205→  - all spatial analyses exclude neural activity when speed `< 2 cm/s`\n   206→  - spatial binning is 45 bins of 10 cm each along the 450 cm track\n   207→  - licking is converted to binary counts and smoothed / binned for downstream analyses\n   208→- Decoder-specific paper processing:\n   209→  - decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity\n   210→  - tenfold cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n   211→  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n   212→  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n   213→  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n   214→\n   215→### Curation Steps\n   216→\n   217→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   218→- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n   219→  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n   220→  - lacking visually obvious transients\n   221→  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n   222→  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n   223→- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n   224→- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n   225→\n   226→**Trial curation rules**:\n   227→- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n   228→- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n   229→- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n   230→- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n   231→\n   232→### Decoders Trained\n   233→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   234→| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not classification accuracy; after-switch generalization is significant only for RR cells |\n   235→| Single-neuron deconvolved activity from task + movement variables (GLM) | FDE: all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` |\n   236→\n   237→---\n   238→\n   239→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   240→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   241→\n   242→### Discrepancies Found\n   243→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   244→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   245→| Cohort size | Code primarily analyzes the `MetaLearn` switch cohort by day, especially the seven switch days | NWB archive contains 11 mice / 152 subject-session files | Paper reports 11 switch-task mice plus a separate 3-mouse fixed-condition cohort | Treat the shared NWB archive as the 11-mouse switch cohort only. Fixed-condition mice are not present in `data/`. |\n   246→| Session count | Code expects one saved `sess` per imaging day; m11 has special handling in metadata | 152 NWB files = `11 * 14 - 2` | Paper states 14 task days, with imaging for m11 starting on day 3 | Counts are consistent once the m11 day 1-2 exception is applied. |\n   247→| Neural stream to decode | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` uses `sess.timeseries['events']` after dF/F + OASIS | NWB stores `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`; no explicit dF/F timeseries | Paper decoder uses deconvolved calcium activity derived after per-trial dF/F | Use NWB `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` as the paper-equivalent neural signal. Raw `Fluorescence`/`Neuropil` remain available for sanity checks. |\n   248→| Cell filtering source | Code assumes suite2p curation has already been applied to the loaded `sess` data | NWB includes `iscell` in `PlaneSegmentation`, but segmentation tables for multipane mice contain ROIs not present in `Deconvolved/plane0/data` | Paper counts are post-curation putative pyramidal neurons | Filter cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` only on ROI indices listed in `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`. This avoids overcounting plane-1 ROIs that are not in the response matrix. |\n   249→| Neurons/session | Code/paper operate on curated cells after ROI QC | Initial naive data count from full segmentation exceeded paper range; corrected ROI-referenced curated count is `155–1780` per session | Paper reports `155–2172` neurons/session | After using the ROI region correctly, the data are consistent with the paper’s reported range. |\n   250→| Multipane sessions | Code notes m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes and pooled for most analyses | NWB sessions for `sub-m17` and `sub-m18` have response rate `31.015625 Hz`, `planeIdx` includes `0/1`, but `Deconvolved/plane0/rois` selects only plane 0 cells | Paper states two-plane acquisition at ~31 Hz interleaved, ~15.5 Hz per plane | The archive exposes only the plane-0 response series for these files. Conversion will use the provided response matrix and document this archive-level limitation. |\n   251→| Trial boundary representation | Code uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` arrays in `sess` | NWB provides framewise binary `trial_start` and `teleport` time series plus `trial number` | Paper defines laps/trials with a teleport period after each lap | Reconstruct trial start/end indices from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals in NWB. |\n   252→| Timebase | Code and methods emphasize ~15.5 Hz per plane | NWB contains both `15.5078125 Hz` and `31.015625 Hz` sessions | Paper says most sessions ~15.5 Hz and multipane mice interleaved at ~31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz/plane | Use a common output bin size during conversion so all sessions share the same decoder timebase. |\n   253→| Trial totals | Data inventory gives `12,216` starts / `12,217` unique trial numbers | NWB trial counts are slightly lower than one paper count | Paper cites `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice in the lick-QC section and `80.5 ± 7.4` trials/session overall | Accept NWB-exported trial identities as ground truth for conversion, while using the paper’s mean trials/session as the stronger consistency check. The remaining ~1.3% mismatch is likely a paper-vs-export counting convention difference and will be tracked in later sanity checks. |\n   254→\n   255→---\n   256→\n   257→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   258→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   259→\n   260→### Variable Mapping\n   261→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   262→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   263→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Crop to valid frame range, select ROI indices from `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`, keep curated cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5`, optionally exclude speed-correlated interneuron-like cells, transpose to `(neurons, time)`, split by trials, and rebin `31.015625 Hz` sessions to the common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size | `preprocessing.dff`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess`, `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Neural decoder signal should match paper deconvolved activity (`events`) as closely as possible. |\n   264→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois` + `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` | `neural` / `brain_region_idx` | Use ROI region referenced by response matrix, then `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` to keep curated cells only | suite2p curation assumption in `make_session_pkl.md`; `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Critical for multipane sessions; do not use the full segmentation table. |\n   265→| `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location` | `brain_regions` | Normalize `hippocampus, CA1` to `CA1` | N/A | All sessions are CA1 in this archive. |\n   266→| Reconstructed trial-relative elapsed time | `input[0]` | Seconds since trial start for each bin | trial handling implicit in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Align all trials to the first frame of the trial. |\n   267→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data` | `input[1]` | Per trial, take modal valid environment value (`0` or `1`) and repeat across bins | session/task logic in `behavior.get_trial_types` / paper task description | Map `0 -> ENV1`, `1 -> ENV2`. |\n   268→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial number/data` | `input[2]` | Per trial, use within-session trial index and repeat across bins | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` creates 0-indexed `trial_ids` | Keep native 0-indexing to match code conventions. |\n   269→| Trial reward outcome shifted by one trial | `input[3]` | Previous trial rewarded (`1`) vs omitted (`0`), repeated across current-trial bins; first trial gets `0` | paper reward omission schedule; reward outcome from trial parsing | User-requested decoder input, not a paper decoder variable. |\n   270→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` + inferred reward-zone bounds | `output[0]` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, `0` inside zone, positive after zone; discretize to 7 bins per task spec | reward-zone logic from `behavior.get_reward_zones`; reward-relative computations in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | For zone `[start, end]`: `pos < start -> pos-start`; `start <= pos <= end -> 0`; `pos > end -> pos-end`. |\n   271→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` | `output[1]` | Discretize absolute position on the 450 cm corridor into 5 equal bins | paper: 45 spatial bins over 450 cm | Decoder spec requires 5 bins, not the paper’s 45 bins. |\n   272→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed/data` | `output[2]` | Discretize speed into bins: `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40 cm/s` | paper and code use speed threshold 2 cm/s | Keep all bins for output even though paper excludes `<2 cm/s` in some analyses. |\n   273→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick/data` | `output[3]` | Convert to binary per bin (`lick > 0`) after lick-artifact handling | lick cleanup in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; methods licking QC | For rebinned 31 Hz sessions, use logical OR within each 15.5 Hz bin. |\n   274→| Inferred active reward zone per trial | `output[4]` | Map trial reward location to categorical `{A:0, B:1, C:2}` and repeat across trial bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones`; paper task design | Infer from reward-zone-active positions / reward events and fill omission trials within stable blocks. |\n   275→| Reward events assigned to trials | `output[5]` | Trial outcome categorical `{omitted:0, rewarded:1}` repeated across trial bins | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Rewarded if any `Reward` event timestamp falls within the trial. |\n   276→\n   277→### Key Decisions\n   278→1. **Use all 152 NWB sessions from the 11 switch-task mice**: The shared archive corresponds to the main switch cohort, not the 3 fixed-condition mice.\n   279→1. **Use NWB `Deconvolved` as the neural signal**: The paper’s decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity after dF/F, and this stream is already provided in the archive.\n   280→1. **Filter cells with ROI-region-aware `iscell` selection**: Only ROI ids referenced by the response matrix are valid candidates for inclusion.\n   281→1. **Plan to exclude putative interneuron-like cells**: The paper excludes cells with dF/F-speed correlation `> 0.5`, so the conversion script should implement this if feasible from the NWB fluorescence streams and reference `dff` logic.\n   282→1. **Reconstruct trials from `trial_start` and `teleport`**: This matches the reference trial definition more closely than relying only on trial-number changes.\n   283→1. **Standardize all sessions to a common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size**: This is the dominant dataset rate and the paper’s effective per-plane sampling rate.\n   284→1. **Use time-varying outputs for all decoder targets**: Even per-trial outputs (`reward zone`, `reward outcome`) will be repeated across bins for consistent `(n_output, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n   285→1. **Infer reward-zone location from rewarded trials and fill omission trials within stable blocks**: Missing zone labels in the NWB export are concentrated on omission trials; the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n   286→1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n   287→1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n   288→\n decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `.manifest`\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`\n- `Dockerfile`\n- `code/`\n- `data/`\n- Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `create_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | LOADING | Creates a `TwoPUtils.sess.Session` object from imaging + VR files, then appends aligned session data. |\n| `append_session_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns VR to imaging, loads suite2p outputs, adds timeseries (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`) and position-binned trial matrices. |\n| `dff` | `code/src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | Computes trial-restricted dF/F with neuropil subtraction, `maximin` baseline, smoothing, and optional OASIS deconvolution to `events`. |\n| `multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | LOADING | Loads saved `sess` pickles across animals for an experiment day and attaches dF/F, events, place-cell outputs, reward labels, and trial subsets. |\n| `quick_load_multi_anim_sess` | `code/src/reward_relative/utilities.py` | LOADING | Loads already processed multi-animal pickle files from the shared preprocessed data location. |\n| `get_trial_types` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Computes per-trial reward outcome and environment morph from aligned VR data between `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. |\n| `get_reward_zones` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Maps scene names to reward-zone coordinates and labels (`A`, `B`, `C`) for each trial, including switch sessions. |\n| `define_trial_subsets` | `code/src/reward_relative/behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Splits trials into trial sets based on reward-zone switches or halves for single-zone sessions. |\n| `get_timeseries_data` | `code/src/reward_relative/glmUtils.py` | PROCESSING | Builds continuously sampled decoder-ready behavior and neural arrays from `sess.timeseries['events']`, masking low-speed and invalid samples. |\n| `CircularRegression` | `code/src/reward_relative/decode.py` | PROCESSING | Paper decoder model for circular variables; confirms the reference decoding was done on neural activity against position-like targets. |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference repo is for **2-photon calcium imaging**, not electrophysiology. There is no spike sorting or ephys quality filtering in this code path.\n- `sess` is the base aligned session object. The README explicitly states raw `sess` objects **do not contain dF/F**; dF/F is computed later.\n- `make_session_pkl.md` shows the first saved object is `sess`, created from raw suite2p + VR files after manual suite2p curation.\n- Cell curation is manual in the suite2p GUI; `iscell.npy` is the curated cell mask used when loading suite2p outputs.\n- `multi_anim_sess` is the main post-processing stage used by the paper:\n  - loads saved `sess` pickle for each animal/day,\n  - computes dF/F,\n  - optionally deconvolves dF/F into `events`,\n  - adds position-binned trial matrices,\n  - computes place-cell statistics and trial subset metadata.\n- For single-channel data, `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)` calls `preprocessing.dff(...)` with:\n  - `neuropil_method='subtract'`\n  - `baseline_method='maximin'`\n  - `subtract_baseline=True`\n  - `neu_coef=0.7`\n  - `deconvolve=True`\n  - optional `keep_teleports` per animal\n- `dff` restricts valid fluorescence to trial spans using `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`. Baseline is computed **within each trial** after smoothing, min-filtering, and max-filtering. The resulting dF/F is smoothed again, then OASIS is applied to get `events`.\n- `append_session_data` adds behavioral timeseries aligned to imaging frames. The pipeline is therefore based on **frame-aligned** neural and behavior streams.\n- `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` is the clearest reference for paper-style decoder inputs:\n  - neural data are `sess.timeseries['events']`\n  - trial boundaries use `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds`\n  - reward-relative position is computed per sample from the reward-zone start for that trial\n  - low-speed samples are masked out with `use_speed_thr=2`\n  - lick sensor artifacts are suppressed when cumulative licks are implausibly high\n  - samples with NaNs in neural data or masked speed/lick channels are dropped\n- Reward-zone labels come from `behavior.get_reward_zones`:\n  - `A` corresponds to zone `X` = `[80, 130]`\n  - `B` corresponds to zone `Y` = `[200, 250]`\n  - `C` corresponds to zone `Z` = `[320, 370]`\n  - environment names remain `Env1`, `Env2`, `Env3`\n- The Fig. 3 decoder notebook uses:\n  - experiment `MetaLearn`\n  - experiment days `[3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14]`\n  - `ts_key='dff'` when loading saved `multiDayData`, but continuous neural decoder inputs are assembled from `sess.timeseries['events']` via `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`\n  - occupancy matching by reward-relative position bin for some paper analyses\n- Conclusion for conversion:\n  - **Yes, dF/F must be computed** to match the reference processing.\n  - The decoder-relevant neural stream is most likely the **deconvolved `events`**, not raw fluorescence.\n  - Trial alignment should use `trial_start_inds` as the start and `teleport_inds` as the end of each trial.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `data/` contains one NWB file per subject-session plus `dandiset.yaml`.\n- Subject directories present:\n  - `sub-m3`, `sub-m4`, `sub-m7`, `sub-m11`, `sub-m12`, `sub-m13`, `sub-m14`, `sub-m15`, `sub-m17`, `sub-m18`, `sub-m19`\n- File pattern:\n  - `data/sub-<mouse>/sub-<mouse>_ses-<NN>_behavior+ophys.nwb`\n- Total NWB files: `152`\n- Sessions per subject from file inventory:\n  - `sub-m11`: 12 sessions (`ses-03` to `ses-14`)\n  - all other listed subjects: 14 sessions each\n- Each NWB contains:\n  - `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries`\n  - `processing/ophys/Fluorescence`\n  - `processing/ophys/Neuropil`\n  - `processing/ophys/Deconvolved`\n  - `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation`\n- Behavioral time series present in every sampled file:\n  - `Reward`\n  - `autoreward`\n  - `environment`\n  - `lick`\n  - `position`\n  - `reward_zone`\n  - `scanning`\n  - `speed`\n  - `teleport`\n  - `trial number`\n  - `trial_start`\n- Ophys organization in sample NWB:\n  - `Deconvolved/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n  - `Fluorescence/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n  - `Neuropil/plane0/data`: `(n_timepoints, n_rois)`\n  - `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation` columns:\n    - `pixel_mask`\n    - `iscell`\n    - `planeIdx`\n- `iscell` has shape `(n_rois, 2)` and matches suite2p format:\n  - column 0: binary cell flag (`0` or `1`)\n  - column 1: suite2p confidence/probability\n- Imaging metadata from sample file:\n  - indicator: `GCaMP7f`\n  - location: `hippocampus, CA1`\n- Neural sampling rates present across the dataset:\n  - `15.5078125 Hz` in 124 sessions\n  - `31.015625 Hz` in 28 sessions\n- Frame-count alignment:\n  - most sessions have equal behavior/neural frame counts\n  - 10 sessions are off by exactly 1 sample (`behavior = neural - 1`), so edge handling will be required\n- Behavioral coding observed directly from NWB values:\n  - `environment`: values `-1, 0, 1` (`-1` appears outside valid trial/imaging periods)\n  - `trial number`: values `-1` plus contiguous nonnegative trial ids\n  - `trial_start`, `teleport`, `lick`, `scanning`: binary-like 0/1 signals with some `-1` outside valid periods\n  - `Reward`: sparse event series with separate timestamps (not frame-length)\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | `118,493` curated cells using `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` on the ROI indices referenced by `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`; `260,091` total ROIs in the neural response matrices |\n| Neurons / session | curated mean `779.56` (min `155`, max `1780`); raw ROI mean `1711.12` (min `315`, max `3934`) |\n| Subjects | `11` |\n| Sessions / subject | mostly `14`; `sub-m11` has `12` |\n| Trials (total) | `12,216` from `sum(trial_start > 0)` across sessions |\n| Trials / session | mean `80.37` (min `41`, max `100`) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Neurons (total) | Not directly reported | Methods report per-session counts rather than a total. |\n| Neurons / session | `155–2172` putative pyramidal neurons/session | “155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session” |\n| Subjects | `11` switch-task mice in main dataset; `3` fixed-condition mice in separate cohort | “switch task (n = 11 mice)” and “fixed-condition cohort (n = 3 mice)” |\n| Sessions / subject | `14` imaging days for task; `m11` starts imaging on day 3 | “total of 14 days” and “for m11, imaging started on day 3” |\n| Trials (total) | `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (for licking QC reference) | “81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice” |\n| Trials / session | target `80–100`; mean `80.5 ± 7.4` across all imaging days | “We targeted 80–100 trials per session… mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials” |\n| Neural data time bin | imaging sampled at `~15.5 Hz` per plane | “sampled at ~15.5 Hz” / “~15.5 Hz per plane” |\n| Behavior data time bin | behavior aligned to imaging frame rate `~15.5 Hz` | “All behavioral and neural time series were sampled at ~15.5 Hz” |\n| Reward rate | `~85% rewarded`, `~15% omitted` | “Reward was randomly omitted on approximately 15% of trials” |\n| Reward switch timing | switch after `30` trials | “Each switch occurred after 30 trials” |\n| Reward zones | A=`80–130 cm`, B=`200–250 cm`, C=`320–370 cm` | “zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm” |\n| Track length | `450 cm` | “450 cm linear track” |\n| Decoder analysis set | `77 sessions`, `11 mice`, `7` switch days | “n = 77 sessions, 11 mice, seven switch days” |\n| Decoder general result | before-switch: RR/TR/non-RR above shuffle; after-switch: only RR above shuffle | “when we tested on trials after the reward switch, only the RR population decoded… better than shuffle” |\n| Decoder spatial extent | z-scored decode `>2` from `−104.5 ± 20.1 cm` to `+152.7 ± 22.9 cm` relative to reward | “range of z-scored decode >2: −104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm” |\n| GLM performance reference | FDE all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` | “On average, the model’s FDE…” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task structure:\n  - hidden 50 cm reward zone on a 450 cm linear VR track\n  - two main environments (`ENV 1`, `ENV 2`) in the switch task\n  - reward locations A/B/C are equidistant and only one is active at a time\n  - reward switches occur after 30 trials on switch days\n  - first 10 trials of a new condition can receive automatic reward if the mouse fails to lick in the new zone\n  - teleport period follows each lap: gray jitter period plus 50 cm tunnel before the next lap\n- Imaging / alignment:\n  - 2-photon CA1 calcium imaging with GCaMP7f\n  - most sessions sampled at ~15.5 Hz\n  - m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes interleaved at ~31 Hz, yielding ~15.5 Hz per plane\n  - imaging sometimes stopped during teleport periods to minimize photodamage; some animals/days were imaged through teleports\n- Neural preprocessing:\n  - Suite2P used for motion correction and ROI detection\n  - dF/F baseline computed independently within each trial using a `maximin` procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n  - dF/F = `(F - baseline) / abs(baseline)`\n  - dF/F smoothed with a two-sample Gaussian\n  - deconvolved activity extracted with OASIS and used for decoder / SI analyses\n- Behavioral / neural analysis conventions:\n  - all spatial analyses exclude neural activity when speed `< 2 cm/s`\n  - spatial binning is 45 bins of 10 cm each along the 450 cm track\n  - licking is converted to binary counts and smoothed / binned for downstream analyses\n- Decoder-specific paper processing:\n  - decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from deconvolved activity\n  - tenfold cross-validation, 90% train / 10% test\n  - train/test either within pre-switch trials or train pre-switch and test post-switch\n  - occupancy is downsampled to match reward-relative position bins\n  - shuffled controls are generated by circularly shifting each cell’s timeseries\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Manual suite2p curation removed ROIs:\n  - with multiple somata or dendrites\n  - lacking visually obvious transients\n  - suspected of calcium-indicator overexpression\n  - with high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n- Additional putative interneurons were excluded if Pearson correlation between dF/F and running speed exceeded `0.5`\n- Reported additional exclusion from this speed-correlation rule: `0.42 ± 0.85%` of cells across mice/days\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Reward omission trials occur randomly on ~15% of trials and are retained as a task condition\n- Licking analysis removes trials with erroneous lick detection when >30% of imaging frames in the trial have cumulative lick count `> 2`\n- Reported lick-artifact removal: `81 / 12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice (~0.65%)\n- Some analyses require at least three omission trials within a trial set\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Reward-relative position from deconvolved activity | Paper reports decode score above shuffle, not classification accuracy; after-switch generalization is significant only for RR cells |\n| Single-neuron deconvolved activity from task + movement variables (GLM) | FDE: all place cells `0.10 ± 0.19`, TR `0.32 ± 0.13`, RR `0.29 ± 0.11`, non-RR `0.29 ± 0.11` |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Cohort size | Code primarily analyzes the `MetaLearn` switch cohort by day, especially the seven switch days | NWB archive contains 11 mice / 152 subject-session files | Paper reports 11 switch-task mice plus a separate 3-mouse fixed-condition cohort | Treat the shared NWB archive as the 11-mouse switch cohort only. Fixed-condition mice are not present in `data/`. |\n| Session count | Code expects one saved `sess` per imaging day; m11 has special handling in metadata | 152 NWB files = `11 * 14 - 2` | Paper states 14 task days, with imaging for m11 starting on day 3 | Counts are consistent once the m11 day 1-2 exception is applied. |\n| Neural stream to decode | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` uses `sess.timeseries['events']` after dF/F + OASIS | NWB stores `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`; no explicit dF/F timeseries | Paper decoder uses deconvolved calcium activity derived after per-trial dF/F | Use NWB `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` as the paper-equivalent neural signal. Raw `Fluorescence`/`Neuropil` remain available for sanity checks. |\n| Cell filtering source | Code assumes suite2p curation has already been applied to the loaded `sess` data | NWB includes `iscell` in `PlaneSegmentation`, but segmentation tables for multipane mice contain ROIs not present in `Deconvolved/plane0/data` | Paper counts are post-curation putative pyramidal neurons | Filter cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` only on ROI indices listed in `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`. This avoids overcounting plane-1 ROIs that are not in the response matrix. |\n| Neurons/session | Code/paper operate on curated cells after ROI QC | Initial naive data count from full segmentation exceeded paper range; corrected ROI-referenced curated count is `155–1780` per session | Paper reports `155–2172` neurons/session | After using the ROI region correctly, the data are consistent with the paper’s reported range. |\n| Multipane sessions | Code notes m17 and m18 were imaged in two planes and pooled for most analyses | NWB sessions for `sub-m17` and `sub-m18` have response rate `31.015625 Hz`, `planeIdx` includes `0/1`, but `Deconvolved/plane0/rois` selects only plane 0 cells | Paper states two-plane acquisition at ~31 Hz interleaved, ~15.5 Hz per plane | The archive exposes only the plane-0 response series for these files. Conversion will use the provided response matrix and document this archive-level limitation. |\n| Trial boundary representation | Code uses `trial_start_inds` and `teleport_inds` arrays in `sess` | NWB provides framewise binary `trial_start` and `teleport` time series plus `trial number` | Paper defines laps/trials with a teleport period after each lap | Reconstruct trial start/end indices from `trial_start` and `teleport` signals in NWB. |\n| Timebase | Code and methods emphasize ~15.5 Hz per plane | NWB contains both `15.5078125 Hz` and `31.015625 Hz` sessions | Paper says most sessions ~15.5 Hz and multipane mice interleaved at ~31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz/plane | Use a common output bin size during conversion so all sessions share the same decoder timebase. |\n| Trial totals | Data inventory gives `12,216` starts / `12,217` unique trial numbers | NWB trial counts are slightly lower than one paper count | Paper cites `12,376` imaged trials across 11 switch mice in the lick-QC section and `80.5 ± 7.4` trials/session overall | Accept NWB-exported trial identities as ground truth for conversion, while using the paper’s mean trials/session as the stronger consistency check. The remaining ~1.3% mismatch is likely a paper-vs-export counting convention difference and will be tracked in later sanity checks. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Crop to valid frame range, select ROI indices from `Deconvolved/plane0/rois`, keep curated cells by `iscell[:,0] > 0.5`, optionally exclude speed-correlated interneuron-like cells, transpose to `(neurons, time)`, split by trials, and rebin `31.015625 Hz` sessions to the common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size | `preprocessing.dff`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess`, `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Neural decoder signal should match paper deconvolved activity (`events`) as closely as possible. |\n| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/rois` + `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` | `neural` / `brain_region_idx` | Use ROI region referenced by response matrix, then `iscell[:,0] > 0.5` to keep curated cells only | suite2p curation assumption in `make_session_pkl.md`; `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Critical for multipane sessions; do not use the full segmentation table. |\n| `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location` | `brain_regions` | Normalize `hippocampus, CA1` to `CA1` | N/A | All sessions are CA1 in this archive. |\n| Reconstructed trial-relative elapsed time | `input[0]` | Seconds since trial start for each bin | trial handling implicit in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Align all trials to the first frame of the trial. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data` | `input[1]` | Per trial, take modal valid environment value (`0` or `1`) and repeat across bins | session/task logic in `behavior.get_trial_types` / paper task description | Map `0 -> ENV1`, `1 -> ENV2`. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial number/data` | `input[2]` | Per trial, use within-session trial index and repeat across bins | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` creates 0-indexed `trial_ids` | Keep native 0-indexing to match code conventions. |\n| Trial reward outcome shifted by one trial | `input[3]` | Previous trial rewarded (`1`) vs omitted (`0`), repeated across current-trial bins; first trial gets `0` | paper reward omission schedule; reward outcome from trial parsing | User-requested decoder input, not a paper decoder variable. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` + inferred reward-zone bounds | `output[0]` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, `0` inside zone, positive after zone; discretize to 7 bins per task spec | reward-zone logic from `behavior.get_reward_zones`; reward-relative computations in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | For zone `[start, end]`: `pos < start -> pos-start`; `start <= pos <= end -> 0`; `pos > end -> pos-end`. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data` | `output[1]` | Discretize absolute position on the 450 cm corridor into 5 equal bins | paper: 45 spatial bins over 450 cm | Decoder spec requires 5 bins, not the paper’s 45 bins. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed/data` | `output[2]` | Discretize speed into bins: `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40 cm/s` | paper and code use speed threshold 2 cm/s | Keep all bins for output even though paper excludes `<2 cm/s` in some analyses. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick/data` | `output[3]` | Convert to binary per bin (`lick > 0`) after lick-artifact handling | lick cleanup in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; methods licking QC | For rebinned 31 Hz sessions, use logical OR within each 15.5 Hz bin. |\n| Inferred active reward zone per trial | `output[4]` | Map trial reward location to categorical `{A:0, B:1, C:2}` and repeat across trial bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones`; paper task design | Infer from reward-zone-active positions / reward events and fill omission trials within stable blocks. |\n| Reward events assigned to trials | `output[5]` | Trial outcome categorical `{omitted:0, rewarded:1}` repeated across trial bins | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Rewarded if any `Reward` event timestamp falls within the trial. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use all 152 NWB sessions from the 11 switch-task mice**: The shared archive corresponds to the main switch cohort, not the 3 fixed-condition mice.\n1. **Use NWB `Deconvolved` as the neural signal**: The paper’s decoder and SI analyses operate on deconvolved activity after dF/F, and this stream is already provided in the archive.\n1. **Filter cells with ROI-region-aware `iscell` selection**: Only ROI ids referenced by the response matrix are valid candidates for inclusion.\n1. **Plan to exclude putative interneuron-like cells**: The paper excludes cells with dF/F-speed correlation `> 0.5`, so the conversion script should implement this if feasible from the NWB fluorescence streams and reference `dff` logic.\n1. **Reconstruct trials from `trial_start` and `teleport`**: This matches the reference trial definition more closely than relying only on trial-number changes.\n1. **Standardize all sessions to a common `15.5078125 Hz` bin size**: This is the dominant dataset rate and the paper’s effective per-plane sampling rate.\n1. **Use time-varying outputs for all decoder targets**: Even per-trial outputs (`reward zone`, `reward outcome`) will be repeated across bins for consistent `(n_output, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n1. **Infer reward-zone location from rewarded trials and fill omission trials within stable blocks**: Missing zone labels in the NWB export are concentrated on omission trials; the task structure constrains the active zone to one contiguous block per condition.\n1. **Use omission fraction and switch timing as sanity anchors**: Raw data show `rewarded = 84.65%`, `omission = 15.35%`, matching the paper’s ~15% omission rate, and switch sessions should change condition near trial 30.\n1. **Keep within-session trial number 0-indexed**: This matches raw NWB values and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot-check: for one single-plane session and one 31 Hz session, compare a raw NWB trial slice against converted `neural` with `np.allclose()` after ROI filtering and rebinning.\n- [ ] Input spot-check: verify `time_from_trial_start`, `environment`, `trial number`, and `previous outcome` on 3 hand-checked trials loaded directly from NWB.\n- [ ] Output spot-check: verify distance-to-zone bin, absolute-position bin, speed bin, and lick bin on specific raw frames loaded directly from NWB.\n- [ ] Trial parsing check: confirm converted trial counts match reconstructed `trial_start` / `teleport` boundaries.\n- [ ] Cohort statistics check: converted subjects/sessions/trials/neurons must match the NWB inventory and remain within paper-reported ranges.\n- [ ] Outcome distribution check: rewarded/omission fraction in converted data should remain close to the raw-data fraction (`84.65% / 15.35%`).\n\n---\n\n## Step Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \\n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \\n\\n## Reference Information\\n\\n- The reference **paper** \\\"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\\\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \\n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\\n- **Documentation** on nwb file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io/en/latest/\\n\\n## Decoder Task\\n\\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \\n\\n### Decoder Inputs:\\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\\n\\n### Decoder Outputs\\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\\n  - 3: 0 cm\\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\\n  - 6: > +50 cm\\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\\n\\n## Target Data Format\\n\\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\\n\\n```python\\ndata = {\\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\\n\\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\\n    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\\n\\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'metadata': {\\n        'task_description': str, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        ...\\n    }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Key Format Requirements\\n\\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \\n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\\n\\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \\n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \\n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\\n\\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\\n\\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \\n\\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\\n\\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \\n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \\n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\\n\\n## Python environment\\n\\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\\n\\n## Decoder Reference\\n\\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\\n- Optional arguments:\\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\\n\\n### Purpose\\n\\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\\n1.
